The finite character identity follows the pinned native changeLevel definition; no equality with the primitive Gauss presentation or actual measure transport is assumed. Existing source findings and qualifications are preserved.

### Tame series after adding one prime level

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-series-prime-level-comparison

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameSeries_changeLevel_prime

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

F_(changeLevel η)(T)=Fη(T)−C(η(q))·PowerSeries.subst((1+T)^q−1)(Fη).

**Hypotheses**

- R is a commutative ring, M>0 with NeZero M, q is prime (Fact q.Prime), and η:DirichletCharacter R M is the native zero-extended finite character. Use the native changeLevel along M∣qM, with q allowed to divide M.
- R is an integral domain, η≠1, and the images of M andqM are supplied units hM andhN. No field, topology, characteristic-zero, p-adic prime or primitivity hypothesis is required for this formal algebra theorem.
- Fη=tameSeries η hM is the existing finite-numerator quotient. Put Y=1+T and Hq=Y^q−1; its constant coefficient is zero, so native PowerSeries.subst Hq is permitted. In the tame p-adic application one additionally has p∤M andq≠p, which supply the two unit images; that analytic application is separate.

**Construction or proof outline**

- The positive target levelqM lets native changeLevel_eq_one_iff transfer η≠1 to the lifted character. Apply the existing tame-generating-equation to both levels: (1−Y^M)Fη=Aη(Y) and (1−Y^(qM))F_(changeLevel η)=A_(changeLevel η)(Y). Convert the residue sums by the finite range identification checked above.
- Specialize the preceding finite-sum theorem to S=R[[T]], φ=PowerSeries.C andY=1+T. Let S_q=Σ_(j<q)Y^(jM). Native mul_neg_geom_sum gives (1−Y^M)S_q=1−Y^(qM). Thus multiplying the first generating equation byS_q gives the required equation for the first term of the proposed difference.
- The constant coefficient ofHq=Y^q−1 is zero. Native HasSubst.of_constantCoeff_zero' therefore supplies the actual substAlgHom. ItsX andC identities, additivity and multiplicativity show that it sendsY toY^q, 1−Y^M to1−Y^(qM), andAη(Y) toAη(Y^q). Apply it to the original generating equation; this gives the required equation for the substituted term. The complete native substitution_one_add and substitution_denominator lemmas verify the exact orientation of substitution.
- The finite-sum identity now makes both the actual lifted series and the proposed difference solve the same equation with factor1−Y^(qM). This factor is nonzero: native coeff_one_pow computes its coefficient ofT as−qM, andhN makesqM nonzero in the nontrivial domain. Cancel it in R[[T]]. The complete denominator_ne_zero and cancellation_identity probe lemmas check the algebraic nonvanishing and cancellation steps.
- Taking constant coefficients gives the factor1−η(q), using the native constantCoeff_subst_of_constantCoeff_zero. This is a formal identity of the existing constructors, not yet a measure pushforward statement. For principal characters the generating equations used here do not hold; their regularized constructor must not be silently substituted into this proof.

**Prerequisites**

- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-generating-equation
- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-prime-level-finite-sum
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.changeLevel_eq_one_iff
- mathlib:mul_neg_geom_sum
- mathlib:PowerSeries.HasSubst.of_constantCoeff_zero'
- mathlib:PowerSeries.substAlgHom
- mathlib:PowerSeries.coe_substAlgHom
- mathlib:PowerSeries.substAlgHom_X
- mathlib:PowerSeries.subst_C
- mathlib:PowerSeries.coeff_one_pow
- mathlib:PowerSeries.constantCoeff_subst_of_constantCoeff_zero

**Acceptance**

- Uses the substitution(1+T)^q−1, notT^q, and the valueη(q), not its inverse or conjugate. Exact twelve-coefficient controls detect all of these mistakes. No primitive Gauss nonvanishing, p-adic measure dilation or interpolation theorem is assumed.

**Tests**

- SuggestedTamePrimeLevelTests.nonprincipal_mass_factor: The constant coefficient of the lifted series is(1−η(q)) times the original constant coefficient.
- SuggestedTamePrimeLevelTests.principal_mass_counterexample: The principal tame constructors at levels3 and9 have different constant coefficients overQ:−1 and−3. Since the principal level3 character takes value0 at3, the nonprincipal formula would incorrectly make them equal.
- SuggestedTamePrimeLevelTests.correct_zero_constant_substitution: The actual argument(1+T)^q−1 has zero constant coefficient, including all positive primeq.

### Tame series at repeated prime levels

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-series-repeated-prime-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameSeries_changeLevel_prime_dvd

Kind: theorem. Implementation: unchecked.

Ifq∣M, then F_(changeLevel η)=Fη as actual formal series.

**Hypotheses**

- R is a commutative ring, M>0 with NeZero M, q is prime (Fact q.Prime), and η:DirichletCharacter R M is the native zero-extended finite character. Use the native changeLevel along M∣qM, with q allowed to divide M.
- R is an integral domain, η≠1, and the images of M andqM are supplied units hM andhN. No field, topology, characteristic-zero, p-adic prime or primitivity hypothesis is required for this formal algebra theorem.
- Fη=tameSeries η hM is the existing finite-numerator quotient. Put Y=1+T and Hq=Y^q−1; its constant coefficient is zero, so native PowerSeries.subst Hq is permitted. In the tame p-adic application one additionally has p∤M andq≠p, which supply the two unit images; that analytic application is separate.
- q divides the original levelM.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Native ZMod.isUnit_prime_iff_not_dvd shows thatq is a nonunit moduloM. Native MulChar.map_nonunit givesη(q)=0. The complete repeated_prime_value_zero probe verifies this with the actual bundled character.
- Insertη(q)=0 in tame-series-prime-level-comparison. The constant seriesC(0) and its product vanish, giving equality of the two existing formal series.
- This equality retains nonprincipality and both level-unit hypotheses from the comparison. It is the step that will remove repeated Euler factors in later conductor iteration. At principal level3→9 the equality is false; atM=1 there is no nonprincipal instance.

### Tame measures after adding one prime level

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-prime-level-comparison

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameMeasure_changeLevel_prime

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

μ_(changeLevel η)=μ_η−η(q)•AbstractMeasure.map(d_q)(μ_η) as actual K-valued measures onℤ_p.

**Hypotheses**

- p andq are primes, including the dyadic p=2 case whenq is odd. K is a complete normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. No independent characteristic-zero, ℚ_p-algebra or nontrivially normed field hypothesis is added.
- M>0 with NeZero M; η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal but may be imprimitive. hM andhN certify that M andqM have unit images inK. hpM: p∤M andhpN: p∤qM are the exact tame hypotheses for the two actual constructors. In particular hpN impliesq≠p.
- Use the native character changeLevel alongM∣qM and the existing tameMeasure at each level. Put d_q(x)=q*x as the native continuous self-map ofℤ_p. The native AbstractMeasure.map is pushforward: it evaluates a testf by evaluating the original measure onf∘d_q. No new measure or dilation carrier is defined.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Apply native AbstractMeasure.injective_amiceTransform. The existing tame-measure-amice node identifies both constructor transforms with their exact finite tame series. The preceding tame-series-prime-level-comparison reduces the goal to the transform of the single actual pushforward ofμ_η.
- For coefficientn, native coeff_amiceTransform andmap_apply express the pushed transform as μ_η applied to the K-valued Mahler test composed withd_q. Use precisely the supplier fine node PadicMeasuresIwasawaAlgebras:L2/mahler-dilation at the scalar(q:ℤ_p). Its finite ℤ_p-valued identity has terms k≤n; native binomialSeries_nat turns its argument intoH_Z=(1+T)^q−1.
- Apply the actual coefficient algebra mapℤ_p→K to that finite pointwise identity. It preserves finite sums and products. The continuous scalar-function action on the constant-oneK-valued test is exactly this coefficient map pointwise, so continuous-map extensionality gives a finite equality of actualK-valued tests. Linearity ofμ_η moves this finite sum and itsK scalars through the measure.
- Native PowerSeries.coeff_map and the series ring-map laws identify the images of coeff_n(H_Z^k) with coeff_n(H_K^k). The resulting coefficient is Σ_(k≤n)coeff_k(Aμ_η)·coeff_n(H_K^k). BecauseH_K has constant coefficient zero, native le_order_pow_of_constantCoeff_eq_zero andcoeff_of_lt_order kill every termk>n. The additive companion of native finprod_eq_prod_of_mulSupport_subset truncates coeff_subst' to this exact finite sum.
- Native series extensionality identifies the pushed transform with substH_K(Aμ_η). Native linearity ofamiceTransform sends the measure difference to the formal difference from the preceding checkpoint; native injectivity concludes. No integral-only Amice equivalence is applied toK-valued measures. The generic field-valued transform lemma is an internal coefficient calculation, not a separately replanned supplier theorem.
- The complete native proof probe checks the coefficient calculation conditional on the exact finite Mahler identity; the roadmap supplies that condition through the named PMIA node, with no assumed measure comparison. It also checks the final passage from exact transform identities to equality of the native measures. Those conditional checks do not claim that the supplier or arithmetic generating equations are implemented.

**Prerequisites**

- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-series-prime-level-comparison
- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-amice
- PadicMeasuresIwasawaAlgebras:L2/mahler-dilation
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.amiceTransform
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.injective_amiceTransform
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.coeff_amiceTransform
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_apply
- mathlib:PowerSeries.binomialSeries_nat
- mathlib:PowerSeries.coeff_map
- mathlib:PowerSeries.coeff_subst'
- mathlib:PowerSeries.HasSubst.of_constantCoeff_zero'
- mathlib:PowerSeries.le_order_pow_of_constantCoeff_eq_zero
- mathlib:PowerSeries.coeff_of_lt_order
- mathlib:finprod_eq_prod_of_mulSupport_subset
- mathlib:ContinuousMap.smul_apply'

**Acceptance**

- Equality holds on all continuous tests and preserves the native pushforward direction. The finite residue controls therefore useq^{-1}a for the mass at a, while test pullback usesf(qx). No source primitivity assumption is required for this finite-kernel route.

**Tests**

- SuggestedTamePrimeMeasureTests.all_continuous_test_comparison: For every continuousK-valued testf, the actual new integral is μ(f)−η(q)μ(f∘d_q).
- SuggestedTamePrimeMeasureTests.mass_euler_factor: The actual total mass changes by1−η(q).
- SuggestedTamePrimeMeasureTests.characteristic_prime_excluded: The required target tame hypothesisp∤qM excludesq=p.

### Tame measures at repeated prime levels

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-repeated-prime-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameMeasure_changeLevel_prime_dvd

Kind: theorem. Implementation: unchecked.

Ifq∣M, μ_(changeLevel η)=μ_η as actual native measures.

**Hypotheses**

- p andq are primes, including the dyadic p=2 case whenq is odd. K is a complete normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. No independent characteristic-zero, ℚ_p-algebra or nontrivially normed field hypothesis is added.
- M>0 with NeZero M; η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal but may be imprimitive. hM andhN certify that M andqM have unit images inK. hpM: p∤M andhpN: p∤qM are the exact tame hypotheses for the two actual constructors. In particular hpN impliesq≠p.

The native AbstractMeasure.map is pushforward: it evaluates a testf by evaluating the original measure onf∘d_q. No new measure or dilation carrier is defined.
- q divides the original levelM.

**Construction or proof outline**

- The earlier tame-series-repeated-prime-level gives equality of the two actual finite tame series under the displayed nonprincipal and unit hypotheses.
- Use tame-measure-amice on each side and native AbstractMeasure.injective_amiceTransform. This transports the exact series equality directly to the existing measures, without a new construction or an assumption that the two continuous functionals agree.
- Equivalently the prime-level measure comparison has coefficientη(q)=0 becauseq is a nonunit moduloM. The direct Amice argument above needs only the already established repeated-prime series node.
- Evaluate the equality on any continuous test or ordinary monomial. The dyadic example raises a nonprincipal character from level3 to9 atp=2. Principal characters are still excluded: their regularized finite constructors do not satisfy the earlier repeated-prime series equality.

### Prime level factors of ordinary tame moments

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-prime-level-moments

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameMeasure_changeLevel_prime_moment

Kind: theorem. Implementation: unchecked.

For every k≥0, the actual moment ofx^k under μ_(changeLevel η) equals(1−η(q)q^k) times its moment underμ_η, withx mapped toK by the givenℤ_p algebra.

**Hypotheses**

- p andq are primes, including the dyadic p=2 case whenq is odd. K is a complete normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. No independent characteristic-zero, ℚ_p-algebra or nontrivially normed field hypothesis is added.
- M>0 with NeZero M; η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal but may be imprimitive. hM andhN certify that M andqM have unit images inK. hpM: p∤M andhpN: p∤qM are the exact tame hypotheses for the two actual constructors. In particular hpN impliesq≠p.
- Use the native character changeLevel alongM∣qM and the existing tameMeasure at each level. Put d_q(x)=q*x as the native continuous self-map ofℤ_p. The native AbstractMeasure.map is pushforward: it evaluates a testf by evaluating the original measure onf∘d_q. No new measure or dilation carrier is defined.
- k is an arbitrary nonnegative integer. The test is the actual continuous functionx↦(algebraMap ℤ_p K x)^k.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Evaluate tame-measure-prime-level-comparison on this continuous monomial. Native map_apply pulls it back alongx↦q*x.
- The ring algebra map sendsq*x to(q:K) times the image ofx, so the pulled-back monomial is the constant scalarq^k times the original test. Continuous-map extensionality identifies the tests and nativeK-linearity of the measure extractsq^k. The complete ordinary_dilation probe proves precisely this evaluation identity for any native measure.
- Combine the two terms as(1−η(q)q^k) times the old moment by ring algebra. Atk=0 the test is1 and this gives the total-mass factor1−η(q). Atk=1 the extra scalar isq, which distinguishes the ordinary moment formula from an incorrect reuse of the mass factor.
- Whenq∣M, the character valueη(q)=0 and every moment is unchanged, in agreement with the stronger equality of actual measures. These are constructor moments; no analytic interpolation statement or weight-zero logarithmic special value is invoked.

**Prerequisites**

- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-prime-level-comparison
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_apply

**Acceptance**

- Retainq^k rather thanq^(k−1): the laterζ inverse-coordinate weighting has a different exponent. The current statement has no analytic positive-weight restriction, because it only evaluates already defined measures.

- SuggestedTamePrimeMeasureTests.repeated_prime_all_moments: Whenq∣M, every nonnegative ordinary moment of the two actual measures agrees.

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameZetaMeasure_changeLevel_prime

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

**Hypotheses**

- p andq are primes, M>0 with NeZero M, and η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal; primitivity is not required. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K.

- Use the existing native character changeLevel, tameMeasure μ, ambient tameZetaMeasure ζ and intrinsicTameZetaMeasure ζU on the native units typeU=ℤ_pˣ. Native pushforward alongd_q:x↦q*x evaluates a testf by pullbackf(qx). The existing inverse weight uses PadicInt.inv, which is zero on all nonunits.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Use the existing tame-zeta-unrestricted-weight identity at both levels: ζ=weight(gK)μ, wheregK(x)=algebraMap ℤ_p K(PadicInt.inv x). Its continuity comes from the existing PMIA padic-unit-inverse-continuity fine node, not a native continuity theorem for field inversion at zero.
- Apply this existingK-linear weighting map to tame-measure-prime-level-comparison. The remaining comparison concerns its value on the actual pushed measure. The supplied padic-unit-inverse-identification identifies PadicInt.inv with native Ring.inverse at every point, including nonunits.
- The native unit criterion suppliesq as an actual unit ofℤ_p. Native Ring.inverse_mul with that unit factor gives inv(q*x)=inv(q)*inv(x), with the reversed order harmless in the commutative ring. Map through the given coefficient algebra. Mappingq*inv(q)=1, using native PadicInt.mul_inv, shows that the mapped inverse equals(q:K)^{-1}. This proof requires no separate characteristic-zero or ℚ_p-algebra assumption.
- ThusgK(qx)=q^{-1}gK(x). For any continuousf, native map_apply and the supplier weight-evaluation identify weight(gK)(map(d_q)μ)(f) withμ(gK(qx)f(qx))=q^{-1}μ(gK(x)f(qx)). This isq^{-1}map(d_q)(weight(gK)μ)(f). The complete inverse_weighted_dilation probe proves precisely this actual native functional calculation for a bundledgK with its defining pointwise values.
- Native measure extensionality and scalar associativity now combineη(q) withq^{-1} to giveη(q)/q. The zero branch on nonunits is preserved throughout; the argument neither replaces it by field inversion nor applies the supplier integral-only inverseWeight_map_unit theorem toK-valued measures.
- On the constant test1 this gives the actual mass factor1−η(q)/q. This remains a statement about already defined bounded measures; no degree-zero analytic special value or logarithmic formula is added.

### Prime level changes on the native unit group

Declaration: DirichletPadic.intrinsicTameZetaMeasure_changeLevel_prime

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

For any u∈ℤ_pˣ whose underlying value isq, ζU_(changeLevel η)=ζU_η−(η(q)/q)•map(v↦u*v)(ζU_η).

**Hypotheses**

- p andq are primes, M>0 with NeZero M, and η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal; primitivity is not required. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K.
- hM andhN are the unit certificates for the images ofM andqM inK. hpM:p∤M andhpN:p∤qM are the exact tame hypotheses of the two constructors. Derivep∤q fromhpN. The native norm/unit and natural-cast norm criteria then show(q:ℤ_p) is a unit; mapping that unit shows(q:K)≠0.
- Use the existing native character changeLevel, tameMeasure μ, ambient tameZetaMeasure ζ and intrinsicTameZetaMeasure ζU on the native units typeU=ℤ_pˣ. Native pushforward alongd_q:x↦q*x evaluates a testf by pullbackf(qx). The existing inverse weight uses PadicInt.inv, which is zero on all nonunits.
- The suggested signature explicitly supplies a native unitu withhu:(u:ℤ_p)=(q:ℤ_p), solely to name its multiplication map on the existing unit group. Its existence follows fromhpN by the native unit criterion, and Units.ext makes the choice unique. No unit-existence or carrier-identification assumption is left open.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Derive the actual unit certificate forq as above and take its native IsUnit.unit. Its value isq by the native unit certificate. If another native unit has the same value, Units.ext identifies it; hence the theorem is independent of this presentation choice.
- Apply the native pushforwardj_U along Units.val to the proposed equality. The existing supplier intrinsic-unit-restriction-section saysrestrictUnits∘j_U=id, soj_U is injective. Use exactly that fine node, rather than assuming equality of unit-domain measures from ambient test samples.
- The existing intrinsic-tame-zeta-inclusion identifiesj_U of each intrinsicζ with its actual ambientζ. Native map_map and Units.val(u*v)=(u:ℤ_p)*(v:ℤ_p) commute unit multiplication with ambient dilation;hu rewrites the multiplier asq. The complete unit_inclusion_dilation probe verifies this square for every native unit and measure.
- Native linearity of pushforward carries the difference and scalarη(q)/q throughj_U. The resulting equality is precisely tame-zeta-prime-level-comparison. Injectivity concludes the intrinsic equality, and native map_apply gives the all-continuous-unit-test formula.
- The complete injective_inclusion_from_retraction probe checks the exact generic injectivity argument conditional on the supplied retraction. The roadmap supplies that condition via the already existing fine section node. No new homeomorphism, restriction functor or convolution comparison is defined.

### Prime level factors of positive tame zeta moments

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameZetaMeasure_changeLevel_prime_positive_moment

Kind: theorem. Implementation: unchecked.

For every k≥0, ζ_(changeLevel η)(x^(k+1))=(1−η(q)q^k)·ζ_η(x^(k+1)), withx sent toK through the actual coefficient algebra.

**Hypotheses**

- p andq are primes, M>0 with NeZero M, and η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal; primitivity is not required. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K.
- hM andhN are the unit certificates for the images ofM andqM inK. hpM:p∤M andhpN:p∤qM are the exact tame hypotheses of the two constructors. Derivep∤q fromhpN. The native norm/unit and natural-cast norm criteria then show(q:ℤ_p) is a unit; mapping that unit shows(q:K)≠0.

Native pushforward alongd_q:x↦q*x evaluates a testf by pullbackf(qx). The existing inverse weight uses PadicInt.inv, which is zero on all nonunits.
- The exponent is writtenk+1 to expose the positive ordinary moment and avoid a truncated natural subtraction. This is an identity of actual constructor integrals; source special-value interpolation retains its separate hypotheses.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Evaluate the ambientζ measure comparison on the continuous testx↦(algebraMap ℤ_p K x)^(k+1). Native map_apply pulls it back alongd_q.
- The algebra map and natural-power laws identify the pulled-back test withq^(k+1) times the original test. Continuous-map extensionality and measureK-linearity extract that scalar, exactly as in the complete ordinary_dilation native proof retained withPR4938.
- The existing native unit certificate forq inℤ_p maps to a unit inK, soq is nonzero there. Thus(η(q)/q)q^(k+1)=η(q)q^k. Combine the two terms by ring algebra to obtain the displayed factor.
- At exponent1 the factor is1−η(q), in agreement with the unit-restrictedμ mass. At exponent2 the factor is1−η(q)q. The actualζ mass, which has exponent0, instead has factor1−η(q)/q from the all-test comparison; it is not covered by an incorrectly truncated exponentk−1.

### Integral prime-level correction coefficients

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-prime-correction-integrality

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tamePrimeCorrection_mem_integer

Kind: lemma. Implementation: unchecked.

If p∤q, then η(q)/q belongs to the actual norm-valuation integer ring O.

**Hypotheses**

- p is prime and K is a nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and bounded scalar action. The displayed lemma does not need completeness, nonprincipality, primitivity, q prime or M positive. It applies to every native Dirichlet character η modulo M and every natural q with p∤q. O is the existing norm-valuation integer subring.

**Construction or proof outline**

- The native DirichletCharacter.norm_le_one gives ‖η(q)‖≤1, hence membership in O by the actual definition of the norm valuation and Valuation.mem_integer_iff. This existing character-bound theorem is used directly, not planned again.
- The native natural-cast norm criterion, together with Nat.Prime.coprime_iff_not_dvd, gives ‖(q:ℤ_p)‖=1. Thus PadicInt.mul_inv gives q*PadicInt.inv(q)=1. Mapping to K proves that q is nonzero and that the coefficient image of the p-adic unit inverse is (q:K)⁻¹.
- Bound the image of every x∈ℤ_p using algebraMap(x)=x•1, norm_smul_le and PadicInt.norm_le_one. In particular the mapped inverse has norm at most one. Multiplying its O-membership by that of η(q) proves the required quotient membership. This does not require an isometric coefficient embedding or an independent ℚ_p-algebra structure.
- Consequently the two scalar parameters used below exist uniquely: a∈O with i(a)=η(q), and c∈O with i(c)=η(q)/q. Choose their ordinary subtype pairs from the membership proofs; Subtype.ext proves uniqueness. They are presentation parameters of existing coefficients, not new wrappers or unproved coefficient-existence assumptions.
- In each level comparison derive p∤q from p∤qM. At p=2 every allowed q is odd, so the same argument applies. The excluded denominator p has inverse of norm greater than one in ℚ_p; the concrete 1/2 test prevents removing the tame hypothesis.

**Prerequisites**

- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.norm_le_one
- mathlib:NormedField.valuation_apply
- mathlib:Valuation.mem_integer_iff
- mathlib:PadicInt.norm_natCast_eq_one_iff
- mathlib:Nat.Prime.coprime_iff_not_dvd
- mathlib:PadicInt.mul_inv
- mathlib:PadicInt.norm_le_one
- mathlib:norm_smul_le

**Acceptance**

- No division operation on O is introduced. The quotient is formed in K and proved to lie in its existing integer subring.

### Prime level changes of integral tame measures

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-integral-measure-prime-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.integralTameMeasure_changeLevel_prime

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

For the unique a∈O with i(a)=η(q), μO_(changeLevel η)=μO_η−a•map(d_q)(μO_η), where d_q(x)=q*x.

**Hypotheses**

- p and q are primes, M>0, and η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal, with no primitivity assumption. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K.
- hM and hN certify that M and qM map to units in K. The exact tame hypotheses are hpM:p∤M and hpN:p∤qM. In particular hpN implies p∤q, so multiplication by q preserves the native p-adic unit group.
- O is precisely Valuation.integer(NormedField.valuation K), with its inherited norm and topology and the native continuous subtype inclusion i:O→K. No field structure, separate completeness assumption, or independently chosen ℤ_p-algebra on O is used. Every comparison is on all actual continuous O-valued tests.
- The explicit subtype coefficient a and its inclusion equation name the correction inside O. Existence follows directly from the native character bound and uniqueness from Subtype.ext.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Evaluate the desired equality on an arbitrary continuous O-valued test f. Apply Subtype.ext to its values, so the remaining equality is in K. This uses injectivity of the actual subtype inclusion on outputs, not any assertion that every K-valued test comes from O.
- Rewrite each included integral value with its existing all-test coefficient-inclusion theorem. The included pullback satisfies i∘(f∘d)=(i∘f)∘d by continuous-map extensionality, so exactly the same field-valued test occurs on both sides.
- Evaluate the existing field-level measure comparison on i∘f, use native map_apply, and rewrite the correction coefficient through its displayed subtype equation. The inclusion preserves subtraction and multiplication. Native DFunLike.ext then concludes equality of the O-valued measures.
- The complete native reflect_measure_comparison probe checks this deduction for arbitrary topological X, continuous self-map d, and native O- and K-valued measures, conditional on precisely the existing all-test inclusion laws and field comparison. These conditions are supplied by the named fine roadmap nodes below; no generic coefficient-extension map is defined.
- Here the inclusion law is tame-integral-measure-inclusion and the field equality is tame-measure-prime-level-comparison. Both are about the same existing constructors at the two levels, with the exact unit certificates hM and hN.
- At the constant test 1, pullback is again 1, so the total mass changes by 1−a. If q divides M then η(q)=0 by the native character nonunit rule; inclusion injectivity gives a=0 and the entire integral measures agree, not only their moments.

- The coefficient is a, the lift of η(q). The inverse denominator belongs only to the inverse-weighted zeta comparisons.

**Tests**

- SuggestedTamePrimeIntegralTests.integral_measure_repeated_prime: If q∣M, the two actual integral tame measures are equal.

### Prime level changes of integral zeta measures on units

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/intrinsic-integral-tame-prime-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.intrinsicIntegralTameZetaMeasure_changeLevel_prime

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

For c∈O with i(c)=η(q)/q and u∈ℤ_pˣ with underlying value q, ζO,U_(changeLevel η)=ζO,U_η−c•map(v↦u*v)(ζO,U_η).

**Hypotheses**

- p and q are primes, M>0, and η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal, with no primitivity assumption. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K.
- hM and hN certify that M and qM map to units in K. The exact tame hypotheses are hpM:p∤M and hpN:p∤qM. In particular hpN implies p∤q, so multiplication by q preserves the native p-adic unit group.
- O is precisely Valuation.integer(NormedField.valuation K), with its inherited norm and topology and the native continuous subtype inclusion i:O→K. No field structure, separate completeness assumption, or independently chosen ℤ_p-algebra on O is used. Every comparison is on all actual continuous O-valued tests.
- U is the existing native units type with its existing topology. The signature names a native u with value q. Existence follows from p∤qM and the native unit criterion, as established in intrinsic-tame-zeta-prime-level; Units.ext makes this choice unique. The scalar c likewise exists uniquely by tame-prime-correction-integrality.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Evaluate the desired equality on an arbitrary continuous O-valued test f. Apply Subtype.ext to its values, so the remaining equality is in K. This uses injectivity of the actual subtype inclusion on outputs, not any assertion that every K-valued test comes from O.
- Rewrite each included integral value with its existing all-test coefficient-inclusion theorem. The included pullback satisfies i∘(f∘d)=(i∘f)∘d by continuous-map extensionality, so exactly the same field-valued test occurs on both sides.
- Evaluate the existing field-level measure comparison on i∘f, use native map_apply, and rewrite the correction coefficient through its displayed subtype equation. The inclusion preserves subtraction and multiplication. Native DFunLike.ext then concludes equality of the O-valued measures.
- The complete native reflect_measure_comparison probe checks this deduction for arbitrary topological X, continuous self-map d, and native O- and K-valued measures, conditional on precisely the existing all-test inclusion laws and field comparison. These conditions are supplied by the named fine roadmap nodes below; no generic coefficient-extension map is defined.
- Take X=U and d(v)=u*v. Use intrinsic-integral-tame-coefficients on every actual O-valued unit test, and intrinsic-tame-zeta-prime-level for the K-valued measures. This comparison is directly on the native unit carrier and needs no new carrier equivalence or restriction theorem.
- Its mass factor is 1−c. A unit witness is still needed to state the full pushforward, but the mass formula is independent of it and the witness is always derivable. Repeated primes have c=0, so the actual intrinsic integral measures agree.
- Finite controls distinguish actual arithmetic μ residue masses and positive ζ moments from separately labelled integral atomic models of inverse weighting and unit tests. Finite models do not identify the unknown arithmetic ζ mass and do not prove equality on all continuous tests.

### Tame measure comparison at arbitrary larger levels

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-arbitrary-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameMeasure_changeLevel

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

μ_(changeLevel η)=Σ_(t⊆S) ((−1)^|t| η(d_t))•D_(d_t)(μ_η).

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including 2. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. M and N are positive natural numbers with hMN:M∣N. η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal and may be imprimitive.
- hM and hN are the existing constructor certificates IsUnit(M:K) and IsUnit(N:K); hpM:p∤M and hpN:p∤N are the tame hypotheses. Intermediate levels divide N, so their positivity, unit image and prime-to-p condition are derived. No independent ℚ_p-algebra or characteristic-zero hypothesis is introduced.
- S=N.primeFactors\M.primeFactors is the native finite set of primes dividing N but not M. For t⊆S, d_t is the ordinary finite product of its natural primes, with d_empty=1. Write D_d for native pushforward along the continuous map x↦d*x of ℤ_p.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Choose the positive multiplier n with N=n*M and induct using the native induction_on_primes. Retain only intermediate a with a*M∣N. This admissibility predicate descends from r*a to a. If a*M divides N, its image is a unit because a factor of a unit is a unit by native IsUnit.mul_iff; p∤a*M follows by divisibility, and positivity follows from N>0. Thus every actual constructor called in the induction has its required certificates.
- At multiplier 1, native changeLevel_self identifies the character, S is empty, and its powerset has just the empty subset. Its coefficient is 1 and native map_id identifies dilation by 1. Nonprincipality is preserved at every positive intermediate level by native changeLevel_eq_one_iff.
- For a prime r and current level a*M, let θ=changeLevel η. Native changeLevel_trans identifies its next lift with the direct lift from M. If r divides a*M, the native character nonunit rule gives θ(r)=0, the earlier repeated-prime measure identity applies, and Nat.primeFactors_mul shows the new-prime set is unchanged.
- If r does not divide a*M, then r is coprime to that level. Native changeLevel_eq_cast_of_dvd' applied to the integer r gives θ(r)=η(r). The new-prime set is insert r S, with r absent from S. This is the exact coefficient to use: evaluating the final imprimitive character at r would instead give zero and lose the Euler correction.
- Apply the existing one-prime measure comparison to θ. Expand the two finite subset sums using the additive theorem generated by the indexed Finset.prod_powerset_insert. For t⊆S, insertion increases its cardinal by one and multiplies d_t by r. Character multiplicativity and native pushforward composition combine the correction term into precisely the subset containing r.
- The complete signed_subset_insert and signed_level_induction probes verify this finite algebra for any module with the multiplicative dilation action, including an explicit downward-closed admissibility predicate. Native map_map and multiplication associativity verify that the actual pushforward action satisfies D_r∘D_d=D_(r*d). The probe assumes the exact prime recurrence; the roadmap supplies it through the named preceding arithmetic nodes. It is not a claim that those arithmetic constructors are implemented.
- This is a finite sum in the existing measure module, not a commutative product of endomorphisms. Only genuinely new prime divisors occur, once each; increasing an existing prime exponent contributes no extra Euler factor. If M and N have the same prime support, the empty-subset formula gives exact equality on all continuous tests.
- Here the action is the existing K-linear pushforward and the scalar character is d↦η(d mod M), an ordinary multiplicative map on natural numbers. Use tame-measure-prime-level-comparison for the new-prime branch and tame-measure-repeated-prime-level for the repeated branch.
- Evaluating on x^k gives the ordinary moment factor Π_(q∈S)(1−η(q)q^k), for every k≥0. Native map_apply and the previous complete ordinary_dilation calculation extract d_t^k. Native Finset.prod_sub identifies the resulting scalar subset sum with the displayed product; the complete scalar_subset_product probe checks this finite identity. At k=0 this is the actual tame mass factor.

**Prerequisites**

- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-prime-level-comparison
- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-repeated-prime-level
- mathlib:Nat.primeFactors
- mathlib:Nat.mem_primeFactors
- mathlib:Nat.primeFactors_mul
- mathlib:Nat.Prime.primeFactors
- mathlib:induction_on_primes
- mathlib:Finset.prod_powerset_insert
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.changeLevel_trans
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.changeLevel_self
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.changeLevel_eq_one_iff
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.changeLevel_eq_cast_of_dvd'
- mathlib:Nat.isCoprime_iff_coprime
- mathlib:ZMod.isUnit_prime_iff_not_dvd
- mathlib:MulChar.map_nonunit
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_map
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_id
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_apply
- mathlib:IsUnit.mul_iff
- mathlib:Finset.prod_sub

**Acceptance**

- Do not multiply the same correction repeatedly along a prime factor list. Exact controls detect this false formula for higher prime powers. Principal regularized constructors remain excluded.

**Tests**

- SuggestedTameLevelComparisonTests.level_measure_all_tests: The finite subset sum gives the equality on every continuous K-valued test.
- SuggestedTameLevelComparisonTests.level_measure_empty_new_primes: Equal prime support at M and N gives equality of the actual measures even when prime exponents increase.
- SuggestedTameLevelComparisonTests.level_measure_moment_product: Every ordinary moment has the product of the genuinely new Euler factors.

**Sources**

- RJW-published, Section 5.2, Theorem 5.7, Remark 5.8 and Definition 5.13, published 143–146 / PDF 44–47. The complete four pages were freshly read in the immediately preceding integral checkpoint during this uninterrupted continuation.. ### Tame measure comparison with the primitive conductor

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-measure-primitive-conductor

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameMeasure_primitiveCharacter

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

μ_η=Σ_(t⊆N.primeFactors\f.primeFactors) ((−1)^|t| η₀(d_t))•D_(d_t)(μ_η₀), using the actual native primitiveCharacter.

**Hypotheses**

- p and K satisfy the preceding field hypotheses, N>0, and η:DirichletCharacter K N is nonprincipal. Let f be the existing native conductor η.conductor and η₀ its existing primitiveCharacter. No new primitive character or conductor carrier is defined.
- hN:IsUnit(N:K) and hpN:p∤N are given. The signature names hF:IsUnit(f:K) and hpF:p∤f for the existing constructor; both are derived from native conductor_dvd_level. The local NeZero f instance is supplied directly by native conductor_ne_zero. These are not additional existence gaps.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Read and use the native conductor and primitiveCharacter declarations directly. Native conductor_dvd_level gives f∣N, conductor_ne_zero supplies positivity, and primitiveCharacter_isPrimitive states that the chosen character is primitive. Native changeLevel_primitiveCharacter says its lift to level N is exactly η.
- Nonprincipality of η₀ follows without an extra assumption: if η₀=1 then changeLevel_primitiveCharacter and preservation of 1 would give η=1, contrary to hη. The complete primitive_nonprincipal probe verifies this argument.
- Write N=f*k using conductor_dvd_level. After natural casting, native IsUnit.mul_iff derives hF from hN. If p divided f it would divide N, so hpF follows from hpN. The complete conductor_unit and conductor_prime_to_p probes verify precisely these deductions. Constructor proof arguments are propositions, so equality does not depend on choices of the derived witnesses.
- Apply the arbitrary-level comparison with base level f, native base character η₀ and level map f∣N. Rewrite the changed character using changeLevel_primitiveCharacter. The resulting prime set is exactly N.primeFactors\f.primeFactors, not a multiset of primes in N/f.
- If N and f have the same prime support, even with larger exponents in N, the subset set is empty and the actual measures are equal. If a new prime divides N but not f, evaluate η₀ at that prime. Evaluating η itself there would give zero and erase a necessary correction.
- This comparison concerns the existing bounded arithmetic constructors. Composite-modulus primitive Gauss nonvanishing, complex/p-adic common-field special values and analytic interpolation remain separate work; none is needed merely to recover these actual measures from the primitive one.

### Tame zeta comparison with the primitive conductor

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-zeta-primitive-conductor

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameZetaMeasure_primitiveCharacter

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

ζ_η=Σ_(t⊆N.primeFactors\f.primeFactors) ((−1)^|t| η₀(d_t)/d_t)•D_(d_t)(ζ_η₀), with the existing native primitive character.

**Hypotheses**

- p and K satisfy the preceding field hypotheses, N>0, and η:DirichletCharacter K N is nonprincipal. Let f be the existing native conductor η.conductor and η₀ its existing primitiveCharacter. No new primitive character or conductor carrier is defined.
- hN:IsUnit(N:K) and hpN:p∤N are given. The signature names hF:IsUnit(f:K) and hpF:p∤f for the existing constructor; both are derived from native conductor_dvd_level. The local NeZero f instance is supplied directly by native conductor_ne_zero. These are not additional existence gaps.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Read and use the native conductor and primitiveCharacter declarations directly. Native conductor_dvd_level gives f∣N, conductor_ne_zero supplies positivity, and primitiveCharacter_isPrimitive states that the chosen character is primitive. Native changeLevel_primitiveCharacter says its lift to level N is exactly η.
- Nonprincipality of η₀ follows without an extra assumption: if η₀=1 then changeLevel_primitiveCharacter and preservation of 1 would give η=1, contrary to hη. The complete primitive_nonprincipal probe verifies this argument.
- Write N=f*k using conductor_dvd_level. After natural casting, native IsUnit.mul_iff derives hF from hN. If p divided f it would divide N, so hpF follows from hpN. The complete conductor_unit and conductor_prime_to_p probes verify precisely these deductions. Constructor proof arguments are propositions, so equality does not depend on choices of the derived witnesses.
- Apply the arbitrary-level comparison with base level f, native base character η₀ and level map f∣N. Rewrite the changed character using changeLevel_primitiveCharacter. The resulting prime set is exactly N.primeFactors\f.primeFactors, not a multiset of primes in N/f.
- If N and f have the same prime support, even with larger exponents in N, the subset set is empty and the actual measures are equal. If a new prime divides N but not f, evaluate η₀ at that prime. Evaluating η itself there would give zero and erase a necessary correction.
- This comparison concerns the existing bounded arithmetic constructors. Composite-modulus primitive Gauss nonvanishing, complex/p-adic common-field special values and analytic interpolation remain separate work; none is needed merely to recover these actual measures from the primitive one.
- Use tame-zeta-arbitrary-level, retaining its inverse dilation scalar. All subsets consist of primes dividing N, so each denominator is prime to p and nonzero in K. At equal prime support the original and primitive zeta measures coincide, including their actual mass.
- The native primitive character exists without any choice of a complex embedding or Gauss-sum nonvanishing witness. The preceding proof therefore gives a genuine roadmap comparison for all nonprincipal tame levels, while the source primitive interpolation identification retains its separate outstanding prerequisites.

### Arbitrary tame level changes on the native unit group

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/intrinsic-tame-zeta-arbitrary-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.intrinsicTameZetaMeasure_changeLevel

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

ζU_(changeLevel η)=Σ_(t⊆S) (ε_t η(d_t)/d_t)•map(v↦u_t*v)(ζU_η), as actual K-valued measures on U.

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including 2; M,N>0 with hMN:M∣N; η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal, without a primitivity requirement. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K.
- hM and hN certify that M and N map to units in K; hpM:p∤M and hpN:p∤N are the exact tame constructor hypotheses. S is the native finite set N.primeFactors\M.primeFactors. For t⊆S, d_t=Π_(q∈t)q and ε_t=(−1)^|t|, with d_empty=1.
- U is the existing native units type ℤ_pˣ with its existing topology. O is exactly Valuation.integer(NormedField.valuation K), with inherited norm/topology and the native continuous subtype inclusion i:O→K. No separate ℤ_p-algebra, field structure or completeness hypothesis on O is assumed.
- The signature names a function u:ℕ→U with underlying value q for q∈S. Such a function exists: q∈S divides N, so p∤q, and the native p-adic norm/unit criterion supplies its unit; use 1 outside S. For t⊆S put u_t=Π_(q∈t)u(q). This finite product has underlying value d_t, and Units.ext proves independence of all choices on the used indices.

**Construction or proof outline**

- For each q∈S, divisibility q∣N implies p∤q from hpN. Native Nat.Prime.coprime_iff_not_dvd, PadicInt.norm_natCast_eq_one_iff and PadicInt.isUnit_iff give the actual unit certificate. Choose its native IsUnit.unit, whose value is q by unit_spec, and choose 1 outside S. The complete unit_family_exists probe supplies the whole function, not just a separate unproved witness for each subset.
- Native Units.coeHom transports a finite product of units to the product of their underlying values. On every t⊆S this is exactly the natural cast of d_t, by the finite-product law for Nat.castRingHom. Hence d_t is a p-adic unit, p∤d_t, and any two permitted unit functions give the same u_t. Complete probes verify product values, the prime-to-p deduction and independence of unused values.
- Use native map_map to commute inclusion j_U along Units.val with multiplication by u_t: j_U(map(v↦u_t*v)ν)=map(x↦d_t*x)(j_Uν). The underlying continuous maps agree because Units.val preserves products and u_t has value d_t. The complete subset_inclusion_dilation proof works over any normed commutative coefficient ring.
- Apply j_U to the claimed equality. The exact supplier fine node intrinsic-unit-restriction-section states r_U∘j_U=id and therefore gives injectivity. This retraction was already planned and its typed interface is supplied by the retained PMIA artifact; no new carrier equivalence or restriction map is introduced.
- Native pushforward is K-linear, so it preserves this finite sum and each scalar. Use intrinsic-tame-zeta-inclusion at both levels and the preceding product-dilation square on each term. The included equality is exactly tame-zeta-arbitrary-level. Injectivity concludes equality on the native unit carrier, and native map_apply gives the all-continuous-unit-test formula.
- The preceding complete native injectivity-from-retraction proof from #4952 remains evidence for the exact reflection argument; it is not recompiled or counted in the current ten lemmas. The retraction condition is supplied by the named PMIA fine node. If M,N have the same prime support, only the empty subset remains and the actual intrinsic measures are equal.

**Prerequisites**

- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-zeta-arbitrary-level
- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/intrinsic-tame-zeta-inclusion
- PadicMeasuresIwasawaAlgebras:L2/intrinsic-unit-restriction-section
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_apply
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_id
- mathlib:Nat.dvd_of_mem_primeFactors
- mathlib:PadicInt.isUnit_iff
- mathlib:PadicInt.norm_natCast_eq_one_iff
- mathlib:Nat.Prime.coprime_iff_not_dvd
- mathlib:IsUnit.unit_spec
- mathlib:Units.coeHom
- mathlib:Units.ext
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_map

- The unit functions are presentation data with proved existence and uniqueness on used indices. Native inclusion is linear; the two convolution products remain distinct.

- SuggestedTameLevelIntegralTests.unit_family_irrelevant_outside_support: Products over the used subsets agree for any two valid unit families.
- SuggestedTameLevelIntegralTests.intrinsic_field_all_tests: Every continuous unit-group test satisfies the finite subset formula.

### Arbitrary level changes of integral tame measures

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/integral-tame-measure-arbitrary-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.integralTameMeasure_changeLevel

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

For any a:ZMod M→O with i(a(x))=η(x), μO_(changeLevel η)=Σ_(t⊆S) ε_t a(d_t)•D_(d_t)(μO_η).

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including 2; M,N>0 with hMN:M∣N; η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal, without a primitivity requirement. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K.
- hM and hN certify that M and N map to units in K; hpM:p∤M and hpN:p∤N are the exact tame constructor hypotheses. S is the native finite set N.primeFactors\M.primeFactors. For t⊆S, d_t=Π_(q∈t)q and ε_t=(−1)^|t|, with d_empty=1.
- U is the existing native units type ℤ_pˣ with its existing topology. O is exactly Valuation.integer(NormedField.valuation K), with inherited norm/topology and the native continuous subtype inclusion i:O→K. No separate ℤ_p-algebra, field structure or completeness hypothesis on O is assumed.
- The coefficient function a is an ordinary function into the existing subtype, not a new bundled character. It exists on every x by native DirichletCharacter.norm_le_one and valuation-integer membership. Subtype.ext makes it pointwise unique.

**Construction or proof outline**

- For every x, native character boundedness gives η(x)∈O. Taking the ordinary subtype pair defines a(x) with the required coefficient image. The complete character_coefficient_family_exists probe supplies this family without a separate O-algebra structure or coefficient-field descent assumption.
- Evaluate the desired equality on an arbitrary continuous O-valued test f and use Subtype.ext on its values. Rewrite both included integral constructor values with the exact existing all-test coefficient-inclusion nodes. No assertion that an arbitrary K-valued test admits an O-valued lift is needed.
- Evaluation of a finite sum of native measures is the finite sum of their evaluations. The complete measure_sum_eval probe proves this directly from their native continuous-linear-functional definition, using the additive evaluation homomorphism. Native map_apply then replaces every pushforward by the pullback f∘d.
- The subtype ring inclusion preserves the finite sum, multiplication, −1 and natural powers. The complete signed_coefficient_inclusion probe checks the signed scalar specifically. Included pullback tests agree with pullbacks of the included test by continuous-map associativity.
- The remaining equality is the existing K-valued level comparison evaluated on i∘f. Native DFunLike.ext concludes equality of the integral measures. The complete finite_coefficient_reflection probe proves exactly this general finite deduction, conditional on the existing coefficient-inclusion laws and field equality; those hypotheses are supplied by named fine nodes, not left open.
- Use tame-measure-arbitrary-level for the actual ambient K-valued μ and tame-integral-measure-inclusion for the actual integralTameMeasure. The included signed coefficient ε_t a(d_t) is ε_t η(d_t), exactly the field formula coefficient.
- At the constant test 1, every pullback is 1 and the mass is multiplied by Σ_t ε_t a(d_t). If S is empty, a(1)=1 by its inclusion equation and the native character law, so the whole integral measures agree.

ζU_η=Σ_(t⊆S) ε_t η₀(d_t)/d_t • map(v↦u_t*v)(ζU_η₀).

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including2. N>0, η:DirichletCharacter K N is nonprincipal, K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. The given constructor certificates are hN:IsUnit(N:K) and hpN:p∤N.
- f=η.conductor and η₀=η.primitiveCharacter are the actual native objects, with f∣N, f≠0, η₀ primitive and changeLevel(η₀)=η. The named hF:IsUnit(f:K) and hpF:p∤f are derived from hN,hpN and f∣N. No choice of a second primitive character or conductor carrier occurs.
- S=N.primeFactors\f.primeFactors, d_t=Π_(q∈t)q and ε_t=(−1)^|t|. U=ℤ_pˣ is the native unit group. O is the existing norm-valuation integer ring Valuation.integer(NormedField.valuation K), with its inherited norm and subtype inclusion i:O→K. No separate ℤ_p-algebra or completeness instance on O is assumed.
- u:ℕ→U has value q on q∈S; this whole family exists by the earlier native unit construction, since q∣N implies p∤q. Put u_t=Π_(q∈t)u(q), with underlying value d_t. Units.ext gives independence on every used subset.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Use native conductor_ne_zero for the local NeZero f instance and conductor_dvd_level for f∣N. If p∣f, transitivity would imply p∣N; write N=f*k and apply IsUnit.mul_iff after casting to obtain the unit certificate hF. The combined conductor_certificates native probe checks the actual hypotheses together.
- Derive η₀≠1: otherwise native changeLevel_primitiveCharacter and preservation of1 would imply η=1. PrimitiveCharacter_isPrimitive is already native. All constructor witness arguments are proofs, so proof irrelevance removes any dependence on their presentation.
- Reuse intrinsic-tame-zeta-arbitrary-level, including its proved existence and independence of the unit family and its inclusion/retraction route. Applying it at the native conductor requires no new restriction, convolution identification or coefficient-field descent.
- The intrinsic carrier is the same U at both tame levels; changing the finite Dirichlet modulus does not change the p-adic domain. The rewrite by native recovery therefore has no hidden carrier equivalence.
- Apply the exact preceding arbitrary-level comparison at M=f, base character η₀, and hMN=conductor_dvd_level. Rewrite only changeLevel_primitiveCharacter. The suggested declaration includes the complete transport proof, verified in the partial signature file against the prior planned statements. This checks the reduction, not the implementation of the arithmetic constructors or of the comparison it imports.
- The indexing set consists of new prime divisors only. For q∈S, η(q)=0 whereas η₀(q)≠0: the first follows from q∣N and the native ZMod nonunit criterion; the second from q∤f and MulChar.apply_ne_zero_iff. The complete new_prime_values probe checks both statements. Substituting the original imprimitive value would incorrectly erase each correction.
- Evaluate on any continuous test to obtain the corresponding finite sum of pullback evaluations. On the constant test1 all dilations preserve the test, yielding the finite signed scalar mass factor. If N and f have equal prime support, S is empty and the sole empty-subset term is the identity. This remains valid when N has higher powers of conductor primes.
- All conclusions concern the existing bounded arithmetic measure constructors. The source special-value identification via primitive Gauss sums, common coefficient embeddings, analytic branches and logarithmic weight-zero interpolation remains separate. Finite atomic zeta masses are not used as arithmetic mass values.

### Tame level Euler factors for arithmetic-character moments

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-arithmetic-moment-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.intrinsicTameZetaMeasure_changeLevel_arithmeticCharacter

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

ζU_(changeLevel η)(κ_n,χ,w)=Π_(q∈S)(1−η(q)χ(q)q^w/q) · ζU_η(κ_n,χ,w).

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including2. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. M,N>0, M∣N, η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal; hM,hN are the exact unit-image constructor certificates and hpM,hpN assert that p divides neither level.
- n,w≥0 and χ:DirichletCharacter K(p^n) is arbitrary, possibly principal or imprimitive. κ=primePowerArithmeticCharacter p n χ w is the existing continuous monoid homomorphism U→K, with U=ℤ_pˣ. Set S=N.primeFactors\M.primeFactors. The quotient q^w/q is kept literally at w=0.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Choose the actual unit family from the tame level condition, as in intrinsic-tame-zeta-arbitrary-level. For every subset t⊆S let d_t be its natural product and u_t the corresponding product of units. Native Units.coeHom gives the underlying value d_t; all unit choices agree on used indices.
- For any normed commutative coefficient ring R and continuous monoid hom κ:U→R, multiplicativity gives κ∘(u_t*·)=κ(u_t)•κ as continuous tests. Evaluate native AbstractMeasure.map using map_apply and linearity. The complete dilation_character_eval proof verifies the exact functional identity without adding a generic roadmap wrapper.
- Native monoid-hom preservation of finite products gives κ(u_t)=Π_(q∈t)κ(u_q). Character multiplicativity, Nat.cast_prod and Finset.prod_div_distrib write η(d_t)/d_t as Π_(q∈t)(η(q)/q). Therefore each signed subset term is ε_t Π_(q∈t)(η(q)κ(u_q)/q).
- Evaluate the finite measure sum using its actual additive/linear evaluation homomorphism; no sum/integral interchange for an infinite series occurs. Native Finset.prod_sub converts the signed subset sum to Π_(q∈S)(1−η(q)κ(u_q)/q). The complete finite_measure_euler probe verifies this entire deduction over any normed commutative coefficient ring.
- The existing arithmetic-character natural-unit API gives κ(u_q)=χ(q modp^n)q^w. Substitute this into the finite product. All n,w≥0 are permitted because q is a p-adic unit. At n=0 the finite character is1; at w=0 keep q^0/q=q⁻¹. Only for w=k+1 may this simplify to q^k. Complete native division probes check both boundaries.
- Equal prime support makes S empty and the factor1. Increasing powers of old primes contributes no new factor. The comparisons are for actual constructor moments; at positive weight the already separate source special-value comparison can be applied under its own common-field hypotheses. No logarithmic L(1) or analytic-family assertion is made at weight zero.

**Prerequisites**

- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/intrinsic-tame-zeta-arbitrary-level
- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/prime-power-arithmetic-character
- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/arithmetic-character-pointwise-value
- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/prime-power-character-pointwise-value
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.map_apply
- mathlib:AbstractMeasure.toCLMEquiv
- mathlib:ContinuousMonoidHom
- mathlib:Finset.prod_sub
- mathlib:Finset.prod_div_distrib
- mathlib:Nat.cast_prod
- mathlib:Units.coeHom

**Acceptance**

- The existing arithmetic-character constructions and APIs remain whole; no new generic character, measure or coefficient carrier is introduced. Integer-ring product statements are tested through their actual inclusion, and degree-zero constructor values remain separate from logarithmic interpolation.

**Tests**

- SuggestedTameMomentLevelTests.field_relative_weight_zero: At weight zero the factor retains η(q)χ(q)/q.
- SuggestedTameMomentLevelTests.field_relative_same_support: Equal prime support gives the same arithmetic-character moment.
- SuggestedTameMomentLevelTests.field_relative_positive_weight: At weight k+1 the quotient factor is η(q)χ(q)q^k.
- SuggestedTameMomentLevelTests.field_relative_level_zero: At n=0 the finite character disappears while the actual coordinate power remains.
- SuggestedTameMomentLevelTests.field_relative_trivial_mass: The n=w=0 specialization is the actual zeta mass factor Πq(1−η(q)/q).
- SuggestedTameMomentLevelTests.dyadic_quadratic_new_prime: At p=2, tame quadratic level3→15, wild quadratic modulo4 and weight0, the new-prime factor is6/5.

### Integral Euler factors for arithmetic-character moments

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/integral-tame-arithmetic-moment-level

Declaration: DirichletPadic.intrinsicIntegralTameZetaMeasure_changeLevel_arithmeticCharacter

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

ζO,U_(changeLevel η)(κO_n,χ,w)=Π_(q∈S)(1−c({q})κO_n,χ,w(u_q)) · ζO,U_η(κO_n,χ,w).

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including2. K is a complete nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. M,N>0, M∣N, η:DirichletCharacter K M is nonprincipal; hM,hN are the exact unit-image constructor certificates and hpM,hpN assert that p divides neither level.
- n,w≥0 and χ:DirichletCharacter K(p^n) is arbitrary, possibly principal or imprimitive. κ=primePowerArithmeticCharacter p n χ w is the existing continuous monoid homomorphism U→K, with U=ℤ_pˣ. Set S=N.primeFactors\M.primeFactors. The quotient q^w/q is kept literally at w=0.
- For integral comparisons O is the existing norm-valuation integer ring and κO=integralPrimePowerArithmeticCharacter p n χ w. No separately chosen ℤ_p-algebra or completeness structure on O occurs. The given c:Finset ℕ→O has inclusion η(d_t)/d_t on t⊆S, and u:ℕ→U has underlying value q on q∈S; both exist from the previously proved tame coefficient/unit constructions.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Choose the actual unit family from the tame level condition, as in intrinsic-tame-zeta-arbitrary-level. For every subset t⊆S let d_t be its natural product and u_t the corresponding product of units. Native Units.coeHom gives the underlying value d_t; all unit choices agree on used indices.
- For any normed commutative coefficient ring R and continuous monoid hom κ:U→R, multiplicativity gives κ∘(u_t*·)=κ(u_t)•κ as continuous tests. Evaluate native AbstractMeasure.map using map_apply and linearity. The complete dilation_character_eval proof verifies the exact functional identity without adding a generic roadmap wrapper.
- Use the actual O-valued comparison and κO, which is already a continuous monoid hom. For t⊆S, include c(t) and Π_(q∈t)c({q}) into K. The supplied coefficient equations and native product laws identify them; Subtype.ext reflects equality in O. The complete integral_subset_product probe verifies this for the entire used powerset, including the empty subset. Values of c outside that powerset are irrelevant.
- Apply the same finite_measure_euler proof in R=O with singleton coefficients c({q}) and unit family u. This yields Π_q(1−c({q})κO(u_q)) inside O, without dividing in O or assuming an independent Z_p action.
- For the coefficient test, the native subtype ring map preserves the finite product,1, subtraction and multiplication. The exact fine integral-arithmetic-character-coefficient node identifies the included κO value; the natural-unit formula then gives the displayed K-valued Euler product. Presentation independence follows by Units.ext and Subtype.ext on used indices.
- The existing arithmetic-character natural-unit API gives κ(u_q)=χ(q modp^n)q^w. Substitute this into the finite product. All n,w≥0 are permitted because q is a p-adic unit. At n=0 the finite character is1; at w=0 keep q^0/q=q⁻¹. Only for w=k+1 may this simplify to q^k. Complete native division probes check both boundaries.
- Equal prime support makes S empty and the factor1. Increasing powers of old primes contributes no new factor. The comparisons are for actual constructor moments; at positive weight the already separate source special-value comparison can be applied under its own common-field hypotheses. No logarithmic L(1) or analytic-family assertion is made at weight zero.

### Primitive-conductor Euler factors for arithmetic moments

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-arithmetic-moment-primitive

Declaration: DirichletPadic.intrinsicTameZetaMeasure_primitiveCharacter_arithmeticCharacter

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

ζU_η(κ_n,χ,w)=Π_(q∈S)(1−η₀(q)χ(q)q^w/q) · ζU_η₀(κ_n,χ,w).

**Hypotheses**

- Use the preceding coefficient-field, p and arithmetic-character hypotheses. Now η has positive tame level N and is nonprincipal, f=η.conductor and η₀=η.primitiveCharacter are the existing native objects. hF and hpF are the derived conductor constructor certificates, and the local NeZero f instance is provided by conductor_ne_zero.
- S=N.primeFactors\f.primeFactors, κ and κO are unchanged by the tame conductor specialization. For the integral formula c(t) includes as η₀(d_t)/d_t and u(q) has underlying value q on S; existence and presentation independence were established in the preceding primitive-conductor comparisons.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Use the exact primitive-conductor comparison at the current realization. Native conductor positivity, unit image, prime-to-p condition and primitive nonprincipality have already been established; native changeLevel_primitiveCharacter identifies the lifted primitive character with η.
- Apply the preceding relative moment formula with M=f and base character η₀, or equivalently evaluate the existing primitive measure comparison by the same finite argument. The wild character χ and its p-power level are unchanged because the underlying p-adic unit group is unchanged.
- At every new q the scalar uses η₀(q), which is nonzero; η(q)=0 there and is the wrong coefficient. Each q occurs once. Equal prime support collapses the product even when the original level contains additional powers of conductor primes.
- Keep q^w/q in the field expression and the actual c({q}) in O. Weight0 is a constructor equality, positive weight k+1 simplifies to exponentk, and all dyadic cases use the same proof. The primitive Gauss/common-field special-value prerequisites remain separately open.

### Norm of the tame primitive Gauss normalization

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-primitive-gauss-norm

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameGauss_norm

Kind: lemma. Implementation: unchecked.

‖G‖=1 for the actual inverse-character Gauss normalization at every positive tame conductor.

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including2. K is a nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. Completeness and local compactness are not needed. D>0 with NeZero D and p∤D.
- η:DirichletCharacter K D is primitive; ε∈K is a native primitive Dth root. Let e=AddChar.zmodChar D hε.pow_eq_one and G=gaussSum(η⁻¹,e). These are existing native objects. No independent Gauss nonvanishing or complex embedding is assumed.
- O is exactly Valuation.integer(NormedField.valuation(K)), included in K by its native subtype map. No independent ℤ_p-algebra action on O is chosen. The norm and unit results include D=1; only the subsequent tame Gauss-series comparisons require D>1.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Native conductor_inv makes η⁻¹ primitive, and zmodChar_primitive_of_primitive_root makes e primitive. For a temporary primitive χ, the existing all-residue shift identity gives gaussSum(χ,e.mulShift(a))=χ⁻¹(a)gaussSum(χ,e), including every nonunit a.
- Multiply that identity by e(−a) and sum over all a:ZMod D. Expanding only the finite Gauss sum and commuting the two finite sums gives Σ_x χ(x)Σ_a e(a(x−1)). Native AddChar.sum_mulShift makes the inner sum D at x=1 and0 otherwise. Thus the sum is D. This is direct finite algebra from the two listed native identities; no finite-field structure on ZMod D or additional generic Gauss theorem is assumed.
- Factor the constant Gauss sum from the same expression. Native AddChar.inv_apply identifies the remaining sum with gaussSum(χ⁻¹,e⁻¹), so g(χ,e)g(χ⁻¹,e⁻¹)=D. Set χ=η⁻¹. Keep the inverse additive character in this product; the same-additive-character version has the parity factor η(−1). The complete weighted_shifts and primitive_product probes verify the exact manipulations.
- Native DirichletCharacter.norm_le_one and AddChar.norm_apply bound every summand by1. Finite induction with the native ultrametric norm inequality bounds both Gauss sums by1. The probe uses the generated additive finite-sum bound from Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Group/Ultra; the indexed prerequisite is the underlying nonarchimedean norm law, not a nonexistent indexed wrapper.
- The bounded ℤ_p scalar action sends every p-adic integer into O. Native PadicInt.norm_natCast_eq_one_iff and isUnit_iff show D is a unit in ℤ_p; map its native PadicInt.inv into K. Both D and D⁻¹ have norm≤1 and their product is1, hence ‖D‖=1. These are the existing tame inverse-integrality ingredients, recompiled explicitly in the native probe.
- Taking norms of the finite product gives ‖G‖‖Gdual‖=1. Since each factor is≤1, multiplicativity forces ‖G‖=1. Therefore G≠0 and ‖G⁻¹‖=1. This proves the arithmetic normalization required here; generic complex absolute-value, local ε-factor and modular-form translation theories retain their existing owners.

**Prerequisites**

- mathlib:gaussSum
- mathlib:gaussSum_mulShift_of_isPrimitive
- mathlib:AddChar.sum_mulShift
- mathlib:AddChar.zmodChar_primitive_of_primitive_root
- mathlib:AddChar.norm_apply
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.conductor_inv
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.norm_le_one
- mathlib:IsUltrametricDist.isNonarchimedean_norm
- mathlib:PadicInt.isUnit_iff
- mathlib:PadicInt.norm_natCast_eq_one_iff
- mathlib:PadicInt.mul_inv
- mathlib:norm_smul_le
- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-prime-correction-integrality

**Acceptance**

- No new generic carrier or named generic Gauss-product node is commissioned. The proof is a finite manipulation of the already native all-residue Fourier and orthogonality statements. The separate complete probe has no placeholder. All roadmap implementation flags remain unchecked.

**Tests**

- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.gauss_nonzero: The actual normalization G is nonzero, derived from norm1.
- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.gauss_inverse_norm: The actual field inverse also has norm1.
- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.gauss_modulus_one: At D=1 the native principal Gauss sum is1.
- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.gauss_composite_norm: For a primitive character at composite conductor9, p∤9 gives norm1.
- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.gauss_dyadic_norm: At p=2 and conductor3 the same norm-one conclusion holds.
- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.same_additive_gauss_parity: Using the same additive character on both factors gives g(η,e)g(η⁻¹,e)=η(−1)D.
- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.principal_composite_gauss_zero: The principal character modulo9 has Gauss sum0 for a primitive ninth-root additive character; primitivity cannot be dropped.

### The tame Gauss normalization is an integral unit

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-primitive-gauss-unit

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameGauss_unit

Kind: lemma. Implementation: unchecked.

There exists u∈O× whose inclusion in K is G.

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including2. K is a nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. Completeness and local compactness are not needed. D>0 with NeZero D and p∤D.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Use the preceding norm-one result to give G its actual membership certificate in the native integer ring O. This is the existing subtype, not a new integral Gauss-sum definition.
- Native NormedField.valuation_apply identifies its valuation with its nonnegative norm. Native Valuation.integer.integers and Integers.isUnit_iff_valuation_eq_one turn the norm-one equality into IsUnit of the subtype.
- Take the unit already supplied by IsUnit and apply unit_spec. The native inclusion maps its inverse to G⁻¹. Units.ext and Subtype.ext make this presentation unique, so the normalization is independent of the membership or unit witness.

### The tame Gauss formula with its normalization discharged

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-gauss-series-certified

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameSeries_eq_gauss_of_tame

Kind: comparison. Implementation: unchecked.

The existing tameSeries η hDK equals −C(G⁻¹)Σ_a C(η⁻¹(a))(C(ε^a.val)(1+T)−1)⁻¹ without a separate G≠0 hypothesis.

**Hypotheses**

- p is any prime, including2. K is a nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. Completeness and local compactness are not needed. D>0 with NeZero D and p∤D.

- D>1 and hDK:IsUnit(D:K) is the certificate of the existing tameSeries constructor.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Obtain G≠0 from tame-primitive-gauss-norm: G=0 would make its norm0, contradicting norm1.

- Coefficient extraction uses the earlier alternating Gauss formula with the same derived nonvanishing. The mass is its degree-zero coefficient; it is unrelated to a claim of weight-zero zeta interpolation. At quadratic level3 the cubic coefficient is+1/9.
- Changing the primitive root changes both G and the finite fractions together. Both expressions equal the existing root-free tameSeries, which proves root independence without changing the character or adding a root-choice carrier.

- p is any prime, including2. K is a nontrivially normed ultrametric field with Algebra ℤ_p K and IsBoundedSMul ℤ_p K. Completeness and local compactness are not needed. D>0 with NeZero D and p∤D.
- η:DirichletCharacter K D is primitive; ε∈K is a native primitive Dth root. Let e=AddChar.zmodChar D hε.pow_eq_one and G=gaussSum(η⁻¹,e). These are existing native objects. No independent Gauss nonvanishing or complex embedding is assumed.
- O is exactly Valuation.integer(NormedField.valuation(K)), included in K by its native subtype map. No independent ℤ_p-algebra action on O is chosen. The norm and unit results include D=1; only the subsequent tame Gauss-series comparisons require D>1.
- D>1 and hDK:IsUnit(D:K) is the actual constructor certificate.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Use the promoted tame-integral-series-map fine node for the exact native inclusion, then compose with tame-gauss-series-certified. The suggested proof is this complete two-step transport.
- Native coefficient mapping gives the integral coefficient and mass formulas after inclusion, retaining the corrected alternating sign. This is an equality for the already constructed integral series, not a new Gauss-based construction.
- Two primitive-root presentations have the same image because both equal this fixed series. The new Gauss unit result supplies an actual integral inverse for the normalization scalar. Root-difference norm1 already belongs to the existing tame-cyclotomic-logarithm-argument-norm node and is not planned again.
- The O-valued Amice transform requires the compatible ℤ_p-algebra action stated in tame-integral-amice. This coefficient-series comparison imposes no such extra action and does not assert an O-valued Amice identity without it. Existing measure and common-field special-value comparisons retain their exact assumptions.

- DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-primitive-gauss-unit
- mathlib:PowerSeries.coeff_map

- No independent ℤ_p action on O is introduced and no measure-space or integral-ring carrier is duplicated.

- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.integral_gauss_coefficients: Every included integral coefficient is the alternating Gauss coefficient.
- SuggestedPrimitiveGaussTests.integral_gauss_root_independence: Both primitive-root Gauss expressions agree as K-valued formal series.

The named finite-field Gauss-product theorem is not applied at composite modulus. The source inverse-character orientation and corrected alternating coefficient sign are retained.

### Primitivity of the tame and wild product

DirichletPadicLFunctions:L2/tame-wild-product-primitive

Declaration: DirichletPadic.tameWildProduct_isPrimitive

Kind: lemma. Implementation: unchecked.

The native product-level character θ has conductor N, hence is primitive.

**Hypotheses**

- p is a prime, including 2; D>1, n≥0, and p∤D. Put M=p^n and N=DM. E is a characteristic-zero field with Algebra Q E. The native characters η:DirichletCharacter E D and χ:DirichletCharacter E M are primitive at their stated levels. Let θ=η.changeLevel(D∣N)·χ.changeLevel(M∣N). All values at integers use the native zero extension at the specified level. D and M are coprime.

**Construction or proof outline**

- Write α=changeLevel η and β=changeLevel χ at level N. Native conductor_changeLevel and the two primitive hypotheses give cond(α)=D and cond(β)=M.
- Set c=cond(αβ). Apply conductor_mul_dvd_lcm_conductor to (αβ,β⁻¹), simplify (αβ)β⁻¹=α, and use conductor_inv. This gives D∣lcm(c,M), hence D∣cM. Coprimality gives D∣c. Apply the same argument to ((αβ),α⁻¹) to get M∣c.
- Coprimality implies DM∣c. The native conductor_dvd_level gives c∣DM, so equality follows. This proof uses no unsupported equality for arbitrary products of characters with intersecting conductors.

**Prerequisites**

- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.IsPrimitive
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.conductor_changeLevel
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.conductor_inv
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.conductor_mul_dvd_lcm_conductor
- mathlib:DirichletCharacter.conductor_dvd_level

**Acceptance**

- Keep the actual native characters and measure carriers, all coefficient embeddings, and the positive-degree restriction. No implementation or supplier completion is asserted.

**Tests**

- SuggestedPrimitiveInterpolationTests.quadratic_product_conductor: For primitive quadratic characters of levels 3 and 4, the product at level 12 has conductor 12.
- SuggestedPrimitiveInterpolationTests.zero_level_product: For n=0 the primitive level-one character gives conductor D for the product.
- SuggestedPrimitiveInterpolationTests.overlapping_conductors_fail: The square of the nontrivial quadratic character modulo 3 has conductor 1, not 9 or 3.

**Sources**

- RJW-published, Theorem 5.7 and Remark 5.8, printed 143 / physical PDF 44; proof, Lemmas 5.9–5.12 and Definition 5.13, printed 144–146 / physical PDF 45–47.
